# Text Elements
Silver reacts with Cl- to produce AgCl hence decreasing
[Cl-(aq)]

As [Cl-(aq)] decreases, frequency of collisins decrease
and by collision theory, rate of reaction decreases for the
reverse reaction.

As the reaction rate is now higher than the rate of reverse
reaction, the reaction rate will slowly decrease so 
the rate of forward and reverse reaction can be equal and constant

pH is increased as equilibrium shifted to the right ^XnZsid7E

 ^6fwyCezV

a reaction is reversible if reactants can form products and products can form reactants.
this can only occur if the forward and reverse reaction can occur, thus activation energy
of forward and reverse reaction must be low enough for it to occur.

in the diagram on the left, the endothermic reaction has a significantly higher activation energy
than the exothermic reaction as the activation energy is = change in enthalpy + activation energy
of the exothermic reaction.

whilst in the diagram on the right, activation energy of reverse and forward reaction is more likely
to react ^kFZmqOFC

relatively low ^Z77wNi1y

therefore, as [H+] is high, frequency of collisions
is significantly higher for the reverse reaction
thus rate of reverse reaction increases.

than the forward reaction, more reactants
are produced than consumed

thus eqm shifts left and more co2 (aq) is produced

thus co2 (g) increases and humans need to exhale
more ^5JRNjThb

[IgA(aq)] decreases

as concentration of IgA decreases, the frequency of collisions
thus successful collisions decrease thus rate of reaction
of the forward reaction decreases.

Thus, as rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, 
the rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate of the
forward reaction. Thus more reactants are produced than consumed.
thus rate of reverse reaction partially increase and forward
concentration rate partially decreases

this continues untill the reaction rates are equal

Equilibrium shifts to the left as the reverse reaction is the
favoured reaction.

yield of IgA and Ck increases thus concentration of reactants
increase and products decrease ^EgH0yKf2

solid precipitate grows in size

concentration of [OH-] increases thus pH increases thus rate of reverse
reaction increases by LCP as system re establishes equilibrium
by partially counteracting the decrease in [OH-] thus reverse
is favoured as that consumes OH- ions.

 ^2VnaIbbm

as water vapour is injected into the system, [H2O(g)] increases

as concentration of H2O(g) rises, frequency of collisions and thus
successful collisions between H2O (g) and CO2 (g) increase
and by collision theory, rate of forward reaction increases.

as rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, as rate of
forward reaction has increased, more products are being produced 
than consumed.

thus equilibrium has shited to the right where the forward reaction
is favoured and thus yield of CH4 increases; i.e. [CH4] increases ^prMZlkKV

silver nitrate reacts with Cl- ions to produce AgCl, thus
decreasing the overall concentration of [Cl-(aq)]

by LCP, system restablishes equilibrium by partially counteracting
the decrease in [Cl-] thus it favours the reverse reaction
as that produces Cl- ions and thus increases [Cl-]

thus equilibrium has shifted to the left and thus
yield of Cu(H2O)6]2+ icreasesand the solution turns
from olive green to blue ^NeYkYmOM

as sulphur trioxide gas is removed from the system,
[SO3(g)] decreases.

as concentration decreases, the frequency of collisions
thus successful collisions between products decrease
and thus rate of forward reaction decreases.

as the rate of reverse reaction initially remains unchanged,
the rate of the reverse reaction is higher than the rate
of the forward reaction thus more reactants are produced
than consumed. 

this continues until equilibrium is restablished where the rate
of the forward and reverse reaction are constant and equal
thus equilibrium has shifted to the left as yield of 
SO2 and O2 increase thus increasing the partial pressure
of [So2(g)] and [O2(g)]) ^gg1MJYHY

partial pressure:: mole present per unit volume ^wzKDzZJQ

when the temperature of the system is increased, solution
becomes greener in colour.

thus equilibrium has shifted to the right. thus increasing temperature
favours the forward raection

when increasin the temperature, system partially counteracts the
increase in temperature thus it favours the reaction which
consumes heat energy thus the endothermic reaction is favoured.

as the forward reaction is favoured when increasing temperature,
the forward reaction is endothermic ^GrhcU20t

as so3 is removed from the system, partial pressure of SO3
decreases thus the moles per unit volume of SO3 has decreased.

as the partial pressure has decreased, the frequency of collisions
and thus successful collisions between products decreased thus
rate of reverse reaction has decreased.

as rate of forward reaction is initially unchanged, the rate of forward
reaction is higher than the rate of the reverse reaction. thus more
reactants are produced than consumed. the rate of the reverse
reaction will partially increase and the rate of the forward reaction
will partially decrease.

equilibrium shifted to the left as the reverse reaction is favoured
and the yield of SO2 and O2 increases ^ZtuxKtMW

		{

			"fillStyle": "solid",

			"angle": 0,
			"x": -404.89819872333175,
			"y": 531.6226429523584,

			"width": 977.0592651367188,
			"height": 250,

			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,

			"updated": 1710157378678,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "a reaction is reversible if reactants can form products and products can form reactants.\nthis can only occur if the forward and reverse reaction can occur, thus activation energy\nof forward and reverse reaction must be low enough for it to occur.\n\nin the diagram on the left, the endothermic reaction has a significantly higher activation energy\nthan the exothermic reaction as the activation energy is = change in enthalpy + activation energy\nof the exothermic reaction.\n\nwhilst in the diagram on the right, activation energy of reverse and forward reaction is more likely\nto react",
			"rawText": "a reaction is reversible if reactants can form products and products can form reactants.\nthis can only occur if the forward and reverse reaction can occur, thus activation energy\nof forward and reverse reaction must be low enough for it to occur.\n\nin the diagram on the left, the endothermic reaction has a significantly higher activation energy\nthan the exothermic reaction as the activation energy is = change in enthalpy + activation energy\nof the exothermic reaction.\n\nwhilst in the diagram on the right, activation energy of reverse and forward reaction is more likely\nto react",
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "a reaction is reversible if reactants can form products and products can form reactants.\nthis can only occur if the forward and reverse reaction can occur, thus activation energy\nof forward and reverse reaction must be low enough for it to occur.\n\nin the diagram on the left, the endothermic reaction has a significantly higher activation energy\nthan the exothermic reaction as the activation energy is = change in enthalpy + activation energy\nof the exothermic reaction.\n\nwhilst in the diagram on the right, activation energy of reverse and forward reaction is more likely\nto react",
			"lineHeight": 1.25,
			"baseline": 243
		},

			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"text": "by removing sulphur trioxide, you are decreasing the concentration\nof [SO3(g)]\nby lcp, system restablishes equilibrium by partially counteracting the\ndecrease in [SO3(g)] thus it favours the reverse reaction as\nthat produces and thus increases [SO3(g)]\nthus equilibrium shifts to the left",

			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710157740486,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "therefore, as [H+] is high, frequency of collisions\nis significantly higher for the reverse reaction\nthus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is significantly higher\nthan the forward reaction, more reactants\nare produced than consumed\n\nthus eqm shifts left and more co2 (aq) is produced\n\nthus co2 (g) increases and humans need to exhale\nmore",
			"rawText": "therefore, as [H+] is high, frequency of collisions\nis significantly higher for the reverse reaction\nthus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is significantly higher\nthan the forward reaction, more reactants\nare produced than consumed\n\nthus eqm shifts left and more co2 (aq) is produced\n\nthus co2 (g) increases and humans need to exhale\nmore",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 293,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "therefore, as [H+] is high, frequency of collisions\nis significantly higher for the reverse reaction\nthus rate of reverse reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is significantly higher\nthan the forward reaction, more reactants\nare produced than consumed\n\nthus eqm shifts left and more co2 (aq) is produced\n\nthus co2 (g) increases and humans need to exhale\nmore",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},

		{
			"id": "EgH0yKf2",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6774.954490404979,
			"y": -615.2757225995058,
			"width": 684.439453125,
			"height": 475,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 1894799136,
			"version": 926,
			"versionNonce": 116999968,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710200924642,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "[IgA(aq)] decreases\n\nas concentration of IgA decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions decrease thus rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction decreases.\n\nThus, as rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, \nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate of the\nforward reaction. Thus more reactants are produced than consumed.\nthus rate of reverse reaction partially increase and forward\nconcentration rate partially decreases\n\nthis continues untill the reaction rates are equal\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left as the reverse reaction is the\nfavoured reaction.\n\nyield of IgA and Ck increases thus concentration of reactants\nincrease and products decrease",
			"rawText": "[IgA(aq)] decreases\n\nas concentration of IgA decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions decrease thus rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction decreases.\n\nThus, as rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, \nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate of the\nforward reaction. Thus more reactants are produced than consumed.\nthus rate of reverse reaction partially increase and forward\nconcentration rate partially decreases\n\nthis continues untill the reaction rates are equal\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left as the reverse reaction is the\nfavoured reaction.\n\nyield of IgA and Ck increases thus concentration of reactants\nincrease and products decrease",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 468,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "[IgA(aq)] decreases\n\nas concentration of IgA decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions decrease thus rate of reaction\nof the forward reaction decreases.\n\nThus, as rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, \nthe rate of reverse reaction is greater than the rate of the\nforward reaction. Thus more reactants are produced than consumed.\nthus rate of reverse reaction partially increase and forward\nconcentration rate partially decreases\n\nthis continues untill the reaction rates are equal\n\nEquilibrium shifts to the left as the reverse reaction is the\nfavoured reaction.\n\nyield of IgA and Ck increases thus concentration of reactants\nincrease and products decrease",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "2VnaIbbm",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6809.200918085908,
			"y": 60.76318976406057,
			"width": 727.099365234375,
			"height": 200,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",

			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710201112583,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "solid precipitate grows in size\n\nconcentration of [OH-] increases thus pH increases thus rate of reverse\nreaction increases by LCP as system re establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the decrease in [OH-] thus reverse\nis favoured as that consumes OH- ions.\n\n",
			"rawText": "solid precipitate grows in size\n\nconcentration of [OH-] increases thus pH increases thus rate of reverse\nreaction increases by LCP as system re establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the decrease in [OH-] thus reverse\nis favoured as that consumes OH- ions.\n\n",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 193,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "solid precipitate grows in size\n\nconcentration of [OH-] increases thus pH increases thus rate of reverse\nreaction increases by LCP as system re establishes equilibrium\nby partially counteracting the decrease in [OH-] thus reverse\nis favoured as that consumes OH- ions.\n\n",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "prMZlkKV",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6799.418271267056,
			"y": 281.8810132821629,
			"width": 667.87939453125,
			"height": 300,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 724816608,
			"version": 662,
			"versionNonce": 1936316128,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710201364699,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "as water vapour is injected into the system, [H2O(g)] increases\n\nas concentration of H2O(g) rises, frequency of collisions and thus\nsuccessful collisions between H2O (g) and CO2 (g) increase\nand by collision theory, rate of forward reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, as rate of\nforward reaction has increased, more products are being produced \nthan consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium has shited to the right where the forward reaction\nis favoured and thus yield of CH4 increases; i.e. [CH4] increases",
			"rawText": "as water vapour is injected into the system, [H2O(g)] increases\n\nas concentration of H2O(g) rises, frequency of collisions and thus\nsuccessful collisions between H2O (g) and CO2 (g) increase\nand by collision theory, rate of forward reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, as rate of\nforward reaction has increased, more products are being produced \nthan consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium has shited to the right where the forward reaction\nis favoured and thus yield of CH4 increases; i.e. [CH4] increases",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 293,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "as water vapour is injected into the system, [H2O(g)] increases\n\nas concentration of H2O(g) rises, frequency of collisions and thus\nsuccessful collisions between H2O (g) and CO2 (g) increase\nand by collision theory, rate of forward reaction increases.\n\nas rate of reverse reaction is initially unchanged, as rate of\nforward reaction has increased, more products are being produced \nthan consumed.\n\nthus equilibrium has shited to the right where the forward reaction\nis favoured and thus yield of CH4 increases; i.e. [CH4] increases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "NeYkYmOM",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6779.054304163304,
			"y": 842.4880105946859,
			"width": 641.87939453125,
			"height": 250,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 1965925152,
			"version": 504,
			"versionNonce": 1937250080,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710201531345,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "silver nitrate reacts with Cl- ions to produce AgCl, thus\ndecreasing the overall concentration of [Cl-(aq)]\n\nby LCP, system restablishes equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in [Cl-] thus it favours the reverse reaction\nas that produces Cl- ions and thus increases [Cl-]\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left and thus\nyield of Cu(H2O)6]2+ icreasesand the solution turns\nfrom olive green to blue",
			"rawText": "silver nitrate reacts with Cl- ions to produce AgCl, thus\ndecreasing the overall concentration of [Cl-(aq)]\n\nby LCP, system restablishes equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in [Cl-] thus it favours the reverse reaction\nas that produces Cl- ions and thus increases [Cl-]\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left and thus\nyield of Cu(H2O)6]2+ icreasesand the solution turns\nfrom olive green to blue",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 243,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "silver nitrate reacts with Cl- ions to produce AgCl, thus\ndecreasing the overall concentration of [Cl-(aq)]\n\nby LCP, system restablishes equilibrium by partially counteracting\nthe decrease in [Cl-] thus it favours the reverse reaction\nas that produces Cl- ions and thus increases [Cl-]\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left and thus\nyield of Cu(H2O)6]2+ icreasesand the solution turns\nfrom olive green to blue",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},

			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 73085664,
			"version": 907,
			"versionNonce": 6249248,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710201776723,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "as sulphur trioxide gas is removed from the system,\n[SO3(g)] decreases.\n\nas concentration decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions between products decrease\nand thus rate of forward reaction decreases.\n\nas the rate of reverse reaction initially remains unchanged,\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is higher than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are produced\nthan consumed. \n\nthis continues until equilibrium is restablished where the rate\nof the forward and reverse reaction are constant and equal\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left as yield of \nSO2 and O2 increase thus increasing the partial pressure\nof [So2(g)] and [O2(g)])",
			"rawText": "as sulphur trioxide gas is removed from the system,\n[SO3(g)] decreases.\n\nas concentration decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions between products decrease\nand thus rate of forward reaction decreases.\n\nas the rate of reverse reaction initially remains unchanged,\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is higher than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are produced\nthan consumed. \n\nthis continues until equilibrium is restablished where the rate\nof the forward and reverse reaction are constant and equal\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left as yield of \nSO2 and O2 increase thus increasing the partial pressure\nof [So2(g)] and [O2(g)])",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 418,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "as sulphur trioxide gas is removed from the system,\n[SO3(g)] decreases.\n\nas concentration decreases, the frequency of collisions\nthus successful collisions between products decrease\nand thus rate of forward reaction decreases.\n\nas the rate of reverse reaction initially remains unchanged,\nthe rate of the reverse reaction is higher than the rate\nof the forward reaction thus more reactants are produced\nthan consumed. \n\nthis continues until equilibrium is restablished where the rate\nof the forward and reverse reaction are constant and equal\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the left as yield of \nSO2 and O2 increase thus increasing the partial pressure\nof [So2(g)] and [O2(g)])",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},

		{
			"id": "GrhcU20t",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6689.143969665794,
			"y": 1830.9501199940946,
			"width": 688.9793701171875,
			"height": 300,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 325028576,
			"version": 609,
			"versionNonce": 267752224,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710201876947,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "when the temperature of the system is increased, solution\nbecomes greener in colour.\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the right. thus increasing temperature\nfavours the forward raection\n\nwhen increasin the temperature, system partially counteracts the\nincrease in temperature thus it favours the reaction which\nconsumes heat energy thus the endothermic reaction is favoured.\n\nas the forward reaction is favoured when increasing temperature,\nthe forward reaction is endothermic",
			"rawText": "when the temperature of the system is increased, solution\nbecomes greener in colour.\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the right. thus increasing temperature\nfavours the forward raection\n\nwhen increasin the temperature, system partially counteracts the\nincrease in temperature thus it favours the reaction which\nconsumes heat energy thus the endothermic reaction is favoured.\n\nas the forward reaction is favoured when increasing temperature,\nthe forward reaction is endothermic",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 293,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "when the temperature of the system is increased, solution\nbecomes greener in colour.\n\nthus equilibrium has shifted to the right. thus increasing temperature\nfavours the forward raection\n\nwhen increasin the temperature, system partially counteracts the\nincrease in temperature thus it favours the reaction which\nconsumes heat energy thus the endothermic reaction is favoured.\n\nas the forward reaction is favoured when increasing temperature,\nthe forward reaction is endothermic",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
		{
			"id": "ZtuxKtMW",
			"type": "text",
			"x": 6662.342076667267,
			"y": 2313.057664484148,
			"width": 708.1395263671875,
			"height": 375,
			"angle": 0,
			"strokeColor": "#1e1e1e",
			"backgroundColor": "transparent",
			"fillStyle": "solid",
			"strokeWidth": 1,
			"strokeStyle": "solid",
			"roughness": 1,
			"opacity": 100,
			"groupIds": [],
			"frameId": null,
			"roundness": null,
			"seed": 419418848,
			"version": 884,
			"versionNonce": 581558048,
			"isDeleted": false,
			"boundElements": null,
			"updated": 1710202058434,
			"link": null,
			"locked": false,
			"text": "as so3 is removed from the system, partial pressure of SO3\ndecreases thus the moles per unit volume of SO3 has decreased.\n\nas the partial pressure has decreased, the frequency of collisions\nand thus successful collisions between products decreased thus\nrate of reverse reaction has decreased.\n\nas rate of forward reaction is initially unchanged, the rate of forward\nreaction is higher than the rate of the reverse reaction. thus more\nreactants are produced than consumed. the rate of the reverse\nreaction will partially increase and the rate of the forward reaction\nwill partially decrease.\n\nequilibrium shifted to the left as the reverse reaction is favoured\nand the yield of SO2 and O2 increases",
			"rawText": "as so3 is removed from the system, partial pressure of SO3\ndecreases thus the moles per unit volume of SO3 has decreased.\n\nas the partial pressure has decreased, the frequency of collisions\nand thus successful collisions between products decreased thus\nrate of reverse reaction has decreased.\n\nas rate of forward reaction is initially unchanged, the rate of forward\nreaction is higher than the rate of the reverse reaction. thus more\nreactants are produced than consumed. the rate of the reverse\nreaction will partially increase and the rate of the forward reaction\nwill partially decrease.\n\nequilibrium shifted to the left as the reverse reaction is favoured\nand the yield of SO2 and O2 increases",
			"fontSize": 20,
			"fontFamily": 1,
			"textAlign": "left",
			"verticalAlign": "top",
			"baseline": 368,
			"containerId": null,
			"originalText": "as so3 is removed from the system, partial pressure of SO3\ndecreases thus the moles per unit volume of SO3 has decreased.\n\nas the partial pressure has decreased, the frequency of collisions\nand thus successful collisions between products decreased thus\nrate of reverse reaction has decreased.\n\nas rate of forward reaction is initially unchanged, the rate of forward\nreaction is higher than the rate of the reverse reaction. thus more\nreactants are produced than consumed. the rate of the reverse\nreaction will partially increase and the rate of the forward reaction\nwill partially decrease.\n\nequilibrium shifted to the left as the reverse reaction is favoured\nand the yield of SO2 and O2 increases",
			"lineHeight": 1.25
		},
